The p5 contract's spellings (borrows struct, &Vector<T> field, func iter(&self) borrows -> VectorIterator<T>, lends self) are unchanged by SL-333; R5 there is the sentence U3 adds its field clause to.\n","created":"1789929984","id":"c55"},{"author":"agent:claude-sl318u2","body":"# U2 ADDENDUM — the two Sep-20 user rulings (branch `sl318u2`, now 5 commits)\n\nEXTENDS the report above; nothing in it is withdrawn except where this says so.\nTwo further linear commits on `sl318u2`, still on main `50a21f4f`:\n`f3bf53b4` (SL-323's flip + SL-327's first pass) and `2a60ff36` (SL-327's fix,\nafter the symptom turned out to be real). Not submitted — U2 submits as\n**SL-318.p6**.\n\n## RULING 1 — SL-323: `existential_dispatch` frames nothing\n\n`frame_boundary` is `cooperative | blocking | test_suspend`. The two\nCONSERVATIVE causes are spelled ONCE, as `CONSERVATIVE_CAUSES` beside the\nderivation, because a second spelling is how this membership came to be read two\nways in the first place (design 206's predicate struck the cause, SL-306's did\nnot, and U4 recorded the disagreement rather than settling it).\n\nThe derivation's docstring now states what makes the move SOUND, replacing the\n\"membership unsettled\" note: design 223 unit 3's refusal fires AT THE DISPATCH\nwhen some conformance body really suspends, and it fires before any framing\nquestion is asked — so every dispatch that reaches `frame_boundary` names a\nmethod whose every implementation is sync. The dispatch keeps refusing a `sync`\nbody, because \"not provably suspension-free\" is `might_suspend`'s question and\nthis does not touch it (`examples/errors/any_sync_suspend.saw` green).\n\n**CONSUMER SWEEP (obligation 4).** `grep EXISTENTIAL_DISPATCH` gives five sites,\nall inside `effects.py` (the bit, `ALL`, `NAMES`, `cause_of_label`, the\nderivation) plus the AST annotation and codegen's vtable dispatch, which are\nabout the CALL and not about framing. `grep frame_boundary` gives every framing\nconsumer, and NOT ONE re-derives the membership — each asks the one derivation:\n`finalize_effects`' `closure_calls_permitted` narrowing; the ledger's\n`free_boundary`, `edge_is_boundary`, `instantiation_is_boundary`,\n`callee_owns_suspension`, `closure_owns_suspension`,\n`_owns_a_suspension_unrecorded`; `_build_frame_ledger`'s OWN method census;\ndesign 260's two fences; `_default_expr_suspends`; this unit's post-lowering\ntotality check; and `build_builtin_namespace`'s\n`_std_suspending_methods_ignoring_closure_calls` seed (whose set is right and\nwhose NAME is now the narrow half — noted at the site). So the flip lands\neverywhere at once, and **totality check (b) stops seeing a dispatch-only callee\nas a `frame_boundary` callee**, which is what the ruling requires of the shape\ntable and the checks: they agree by reading, not by a second edit.\n\n`closure_only` stays narrow (it names ONE source, which is what a diagnostic\nwould say) and `conservative_only` joins the derivations as the row for \"might\nsuspend, owns no park\" — the brief's rule is that a consumer needing a subset no\nrow names adds a row rather than combining bits, and widening `closure_only`\nwould have left its name covering one member of a two-member set.\n\n**THE AFFECTED-BODY CENSUS the ruling was waiting on.** The whole-corpus\n`--emit-frame-ledger` sweep, rerun over 2640 programs and diffed against main\n`50a21f4f` on the `boundary` column alone:\n\n    files compared: 2640\n    FRAME rows whose `boundary` column CHANGED: 0\n\nZero, and the reason is worth stating rather than leaving as a null result: the\nONLY frame rows anywhere in the corpus that carry `existential_dispatch` are the\ntwo in the K115 pin, and that program did not compile before the flip, so it\ncontributed no rows to the pre-sweep at all. Its dump now reads\n\n    FRAME root$m$            causes={test_suspend, existential_dispatch}  boundary=yes  decision=framed\n    FRAME via_existential$m$ causes={existential_dispatch}                boundary=no   decision=no-frame-owed\n\nwhich is the ruled answer exactly: the erased helper frames nothing, and `root`\nis framed on `test_suspend`, not on the dispatch. No other body in the corpus,\nin blade, in the libs or in the read-only sawtracker sources is framed on a\ndispatch, so the flip's blast radius is one program and it is the pin.\n\n`examples/coro_existential_only_body_is_not_framed.saw` loses its XFAIL and\npasses with its EXPECT directives as written (`__Frame_via_existential` absent,\n`__Frame_root` present, prints 6). K115's INDEX row is rewritten to the ruled\nanswer — frames NOTHING, like K114 — with what makes it sound, what it used to\ncost (an ordinary erased-parameter helper was uncallable from a driven body),\nand the `CONSERVATIVE_CAUSES` / `conservative_only` note. LANGUAGE_SPEC and the\nsaw-lang skill carry the dispatch's place among the causes, per design 125.\n\n## RULING 2 — SL-327: REPRODUCED, and fixed at the keying\n\n**My first pass reported this as non-reproducing. That was wrong, and the way it\nwas wrong is the finding.** I probed main and the branch with a cold std cache\nand a warm one, in four program shapes, and every one reported both violations\nat the first settling — so I wrote the invariant up as holding by construction.\nThen the two-violation test I had just added PASSED in isolation and FAILED in\nthe full suite twenty minutes later, same tree, same commit. The symptom is\nCACHE-STATE DEPENDENT: a freshly built std pair agrees with itself; a RESTORED\none need not.\n\n**THE MEASUREMENT.** Five of the eight blobs in `.build/stdcache/` held a\ndesign-206 seed table keyed to an AST that was not the one stored beside them:\n\n    36a50d1bb336c63b  seed=  1  ast methods= 539  bound=483426  keys NOT in the AST:  1\n    36f9e7d048727f06  seed= 18  ast methods= 625  bound= 37997  keys NOT in the AST:  0\n    467c0ab852b876c2  seed=  1  ast methods= 539  bound=366890  keys NOT in the AST:  1\n    59888238fe8cd9b2  seed=  1  ast methods= 539  bound=513954  keys NOT in the AST:  1\n    76855a735da73ee3  seed= 18  ast methods= 625  bound= 37997  keys NOT in the AST:  0\n    8161b3231cfa6edb  seed= 18  ast methods= 625  bound=499979  keys NOT in the AST: 18\n    ab621f03bba1f87e  seed=  1  ast methods= 539  bound=182671  keys NOT in the AST:  1\n    aed00998779eb9f9  seed=  0  ast methods=  12  bound=733519  keys NOT in the AST:  0\n\n`Command.output` keyed **20761** in the table and **62190** in both the restored\nAST and the namespace symbol the entry graph's edges resolve through — the\nissue's own two numbers, now from the blob rather than from a round dump. Every\ninconsistent blob was written by a process whose `node_id` bound was an order of\nmagnitude past a fresh std build's (37997 against 182671-733519), i.e. by a\nprocess that had already compiled something — which is exactly the state\n`_prepared_builtins`' own docstring says the pair must not be stored from (\"has\nto STAY pristine long enough to be stored\"). One blob holds an AST of TWELVE\nmethods beside a full std namespace. A pickle round trip preserves `node_id`\n(probed directly), so the inconsistency is at the STORE, not the restore.\n\n**WHY IT IS QUIET**, and why the pairing is the test: the seed leaf is minted\nunder a key no edge carries, the violation is decided at a LATER settling, and\nthe pipeline stops after a settling that reported errors — so any compile with\nanother error in it never reaches that round and loses the diagnostic. Not a\nmiscompile; a clean program always reaches the later round. A single violation\ncan never show it, because alone it always reaches whichever round decides it.\n\n**TWO KEYING FIXES. The settling loop is untouched.**\n\n  * `stdcache._pair_is_consistent` — `store` declines to publish a pair whose\n    seed keys are not in the AST it is about to pickle beside them. One walk of\n    the std extensions per cache MISS. Declining costs one cold std build;\n    publishing costs every later compile a diagnostic, silently, until the blob\n    ages out.\n  * `sawc._rekeyed_std_seed` — the handover re-keys a restored table onto the\n    AST this compile actually holds, so blobs already on developers' disks are\n    harmless too. The fallback key is `(owner, method)`, which is the\n    granularity every OTHER std suspension census already uses (design 84: a std\n    method has no node in the entry graph, so a name is all that crosses) — the\n    node id exists here only so an effect EDGE can find the leaf. An overloaded\n    pair re-keys onto every matching declaration, the conservative direction. A\n    table whose keys are all present is returned unchanged, so the ordinary path\n    pays one walk and nothing else.\n\n**PROVEN**, against the real inconsistent blob (kept at\n`.build/scratch/badblob/8161b3231cfa6edb.blob` for the lead):\n\n    restored blob: seed=18  keys NOT in the AST: 18\n    after re-key : seed=18  keys NOT in the AST: 0\n    `Command.output` namespace symbol id = 487780  seeded before=False  seeded after=True\n    REPAIRED\n\nand end to end: with that blob on disk, the two-violation program reports BOTH\nerrors on three consecutive runs, where before it reported one.\n\n**Test:** `examples/errors/thread_body_reports_both_violations.saw` — a\n`Thread.spawn { boundary() }` (a cooperative primitive, decided directly at the\nfirst settling) beside a `Thread.spawn { run_job(...) }` reaching\n`Command.output` through four hops. It asserts both messages AND\n`2 error(s) generated`. K112 keeps its own file, as the ruling requires: its cell\nasserts the MESSAGE, and a message is only emitted for the first error a\nsettling reports.\n\n`_effect_seed_std_methods`' docstring carries the measurement and what enforces\nthe invariant now — replacing the paragraph my first pass wrote claiming the\ngenerations agree by construction. They did not.\n\n## Validation, re-run\n\n**(a) corpus ledger diff** (2640 programs, main `50a21f4f` vs the branch):\n\n    files compared: 2640\n      2571  identical\n        64  decision=declined -> decision=template\n        59  files whose only change is those renames\n         5  only in post (the new test files)\n         2  refused -> compiled\n         2  refusal message changed\n         1  compiled -> refused\n    RESIDUE (differences needing a name): 10 — all ten named\n\nThe two new residue lines over the first report are\n`examples/errors/thread_body_reports_both_violations.saw` (NEW) and\n`examples/coro_existential_only_body_is_not_framed.saw` (REFUSED -> COMPILED,\nSL-323's pin). The other eight are unchanged from the first report.\n`boundary`-column changes: **0** (the census above).\n\n**(b) IR byte-compare** (design-220 manifests, main `50a21f4f` vs the branch,\nthe checkout root normalized in `!DIFile` on both sides):\n\n    base entries: 1579   branch entries: 1582   compared: 1578\n    only in base: 1      only in branch: 4      artifact missing: 0\n    BYTE MISMATCHES: 0\n\n    only in base:   examples/vector_closure_suspending_transform.saw   (now a refusal test)\n    only in branch: examples/conformance/K118_...saw                   (new)\n    only in branch: examples/coro_entry_template_chain_...saw          (new)\n    only in branch: examples/coro_existential_only_body_is_not_framed.saw  (SL-323's pin, which now compiles)\n    only in branch: examples/coro_generic_call_in_an_expression_position.saw  (SL-326's pin, which now compiles)\n\nEvery program that compiles on both sides still produces byte-identical\noptimized IR — including after the framing flip, which is the sharpest evidence\nthat its blast radius is the one program the census named.\n\n**(c) outcomes:** suite **2574 passed / 10 xfailed** (main: 2567/12). Against the\nfirst report: +1 pass and -1 xfail from SL-323's pin flipping, +1 pass from the\nSL-327 test. Zero XPASS, zero uncited. U3 dispatches after SL-333 lands.","created":"1789948207","id":"c60"},{"author":"agent:codex","body":"Requested changes on patch SL-318.p6 (revision 1)\n\nReviewed SL-318.p6 revision 1 (base bfe4e588, applied/tested 5a9c7481). Request changes: two holes remain in U2's totality/refusal guarantees, plus an identity gap in SL-327's cache repair.\n\n[P1] Run the undriven-closure refusal even when no frame ledger is built.\nsawc/coro_transform.py:12460-12461; _refuse_undriven_suspending_closures is called only later, at :12862.\nThe new closure refusal is still conditional on a coroutine root existing. _build_frame_ledger returns None when there are no driven/spawned roots and main does not own a framing cause; transform_program returns before the closure scan. This is precisely relevant to the imported helper whose enclosing function carries only a conservative cause.\n\nReproduced using the patch's own sl316closure fixture:\n  import sl316closure.{each_yield_public}\n  func main() {\n      let values: Vector<Int> = [1, 2]\n      print(each_yield_public(&values))\n  }\nCompile with --module-path sl316closure=examples/modules/sl316closure: succeeds and prints 2 despite the yield_now() inside the imported closure. Add an unrelated marker() containing __saw_suspend() and print(__saw_drive_steps(marker())) to main: the same imported closure is now correctly refused at lib.saw:23:17. Whether the unsupported closure is rejected must not depend on an unrelated drive site. Make the closure/effect validation available independently of frame construction and add the no-driven-root counterpart to the existing refusal test. This is an unclosed SL-316 cell, not a request to support suspending closures.\n\n[P1] Check genuine method calls in post-lowering totality.\nsawc/coro_transform.py:11589-11602.\nThe MethodCall loop asks ledger.key_of(mc), which intentionally returns None for a genuine method, and then checks only is_chan_recv. It never asks for the method's recorded suspension/target. Consequently a plain instance/static method call left in a generated resume body is invisible to check (b).\n\nIndependent fault injection on examples/coro_nested_suspend_method.saw: captured the real typed c.step() node before lowering and inserted a copy into a generated resume body immediately before _verify_resume_totality. The frozen ledger answered MethodTarget(kind='embed', frame_key='Counter_step', owner='Counter', is_static=False), and key_of returned None. _verify_resume_totality returned successfully. The probe removed the injected node afterward so the control compiler could finish; this establishes that the invariant misses the injected method call, not that the subsequent re-typecheck accepts it. The committed gate exercises only a free FunctionCall, so it passes despite this gap.\n\nRoute genuine methods through the ledger's appropriate method decision, distinguishing synthesized resume/release/runtime calls, and add method-family negative controls beside the free-call injection (instance, static and generic/stamped where applicable). The promised post-lowering guarantee covers all frame-boundary calls, not just free/module-qualified calls and channel receives.\n\n[P2] Validate cached seed identity, not numeric-ID membership alone.\nsawc/stdcache.py:210-215; sawc/sawc.py:805-810.\n_pair_is_consistent accepts a seed key whenever that integer exists among AST methods. _rekeyed_std_seed has the same fast path and preserves an existing integer unconditionally. But the defect being repaired is that the table and AST can belong to different node-ID generations: an integer can name the WRONG method as well as no method. The code already builds by_id -> (owner, name), but never compares that identity with the seed entry.\n\nDirect synthetic pairing probe: AST Command.output has node_id 20 and Command.status has node_id 10; the stale seed table has key 10 and display identity `Command.output`. _pair_is_consistent returns True and _rekeyed_std_seed returns the table unchanged, leaving output unseeded and attributing its causes to status. This is a fault-injection test of the repair boundary; I did not find such a colliding blob on this machine. Check identity before trusting a surviving integer, re-key mismatches using a stable identity, and gate both missing-ID and wrong-method-ID cases. Also make unresolvable seed identities trigger an explicit cache fallback/invariant rather than silently dropping their entries.\n\nValidation: independently ran corototality and corodiscovery (both pass), plus seven focused runtime/refusal tests (7/7: K118, qualified generic embedding, entry-template chain, generic expression call, existential-only framing, imported suspending closure, paired thread diagnostics). Ran the additional probes above and git diff --check (clean). Server reports 2574 passed / 10 expected failures and all 36 freestanding cases passing; the author/lead report a full 30-stage battery and zero IR mismatches across common successful programs. Those results support the exercised cases but do not cover these gaps. No implementation changes made by this review.","created":"1789949784","id":"c61"},{"author":"agent:claude-sawlang","body":"LEAD ACK of SL-318.p6 r1 request-changes (codex r1; Sep 20 night): all three accepted as filed — [P1] the SL-316 closure refusal must not depend on a frame ledger existing (no driven root → no refusal today; codex's sl316closure repro); [P1] post-lowering totality check (b) never asks the ledger's METHOD decision, so an injected genuine method call in a resume body is invisible (codex's Counter_step injection); [P2] the SL-327 re-keying validates numeric membership, not identity — a surviving integer can name the WRONG method; unresolvable seed identities must fail loudly, not drop. The original U2 agent is gone (session limit); a fresh Opus agent (agent:claude-sl318u2b) resumes in the sl318u2 worktree on tip 9487b190 with the review as its worklist: three linear commits, one per finding, each with the negative control codex described (no-driven-root closure cell; method-family injections — instance, static, generic/stamped — beside the free-call one in the corototality lane; wrong-method-id + missing-id + unresolvable-identity cases for the seed pair), per-commit gate each, terminal battery on the tip, then the lead re-validates with codex's own repros and revises p6 to r2.\n","created":"1789951158","id":"c62"},{"author":"agent:claude-sl318u2b","body":"design 275 U2 — codex's SL-318.p6 r1 request-changes: all three findings fixed, on branch `sl318u2` (three linear commits on 9487b190). Not submitted; the lead re-validates and revises p6.\n\nCOMMITS\n- `49e0ebcf` SL-316's refusal no longer depends on a frame ledger existing (P1-a)\n- `acc58988` totality check (b) asks the ledger's METHOD decision (P1-b)\n- `9e9890e5` the std seed repair validates IDENTITY, not membership (P2)\n\n--------------------------------------------------------------------\n[P1-a] THE SL-316 CLOSURE REFUSAL, INDEPENDENT OF FRAME CONSTRUCTION\n\nMECHANISM — two gates, not one. `_build_frame_ledger` answered `None` for a\nprogram with no driven/spawned root and a non-suspending `main`, and\n`transform_program` returned before `_refuse_undriven_suspending_closures`. And\none level up, `sawc.py:2029`'s `driven` test never ENTERED the transform for\nsuch a program at all, so widening the ledger alone changed nothing (verified:\nafter the first attempt both of codex's repros still compiled and printed `2`).\n\nFIX. (1) `_build_frame_ledger` returns a ROOTLESS ledger instead of `None` —\nthe body tables (imported free bodies included) and the cause set, no frames, no\nsites, frozen; `has_coroutine_root` is the recorded fact beside `main_suspends`.\n(2) The transform's entry condition gains its second half,\n`_has_suspending_closure_body`, computed in `finalize_effects` off the same\n`classify_suspensions` run that already computes `_main_suspends`. ONE WALK,\ntwo mutually exclusive entry points — after every frame is built when there is a\nroot (so a closure in a driven body keeps the message naming its driven\nfunction), immediately out of the rootless ledger when there is not. The shape\ntable's `ClosureExpr` REFUSE row is still the one rejector.\n\nNEGATIVE CONTROLS + PROOF. Pre-fix, codex's repro compiled and printed `2`; the\nENTRY-module twin did too (the old test header's claim that the entry twin \"was\nalways refused\" holds only when something in the program is framed). Post-fix\nboth are refused at the closure — `lib.saw:23:17` for the imported one.\n  - `examples/errors/coro_imported_closure_body_suspends_with_no_driven_root.saw`\n    (codex's repro verbatim, `--module-path sl316closure=...`)\n  - `examples/errors/coro_closure_body_suspends_with_no_driven_root.saw` (the\n    entry twin)\n  - conformance row K120 updated FIRST (obligation 3), naming both files and the\n    no-root cell.\n\nA LATENT CELL THIS EXPOSED, fixed in the same commit rather than worked around.\nA `Thread.spawn { ... }` brace IS a closure, so the SL-316 walk looks at it —\nand it asked `frame_boundary`, which the blocking-extern cause satisfies. So\n`Thread.spawn { blocking_call() }` was refused as \"a closure body is not driven\"\nin any program that ALSO held a coroutine root, contradicting design 242 ruling\n9 and conformance row K84. Verified against the inherited tip 9487b190 (i.e.\nagainst p6 r1 as submitted): a 13-line program with one thread brace and one\n`__saw_drive_steps` is refused there. K84's own program has no root, so the\ntransform was never entered for it and the contradiction was invisible; widening\nthe gate surfaced it as a suite failure. The two claims are one question about\none closure, so they are one funnel now — `effects.closure_body_owns_suspension`,\nread by both the entry gate and `FrameLedger.closure_owns_suspension` — and a\nthread brace asks ruling 9's narrower `refused_in_thread_body` there (where that\nsays yes, the sync violation is already reported and the compile stops before the\ntransform, so the closure walk never speaks second). Pinned by\n`examples/thread_spawn_blocking_beside_a_driven_root.saw`; K84's row names it.\nNo tracker issue filed: the defect exists only on this unmerged branch.\n\nCONSUMER SWEEP (obligation 2 — this flips \"compiles silently\" to \"refused\").\n`corodiff`'s `closure_from_driven` context put the generated body INSIDE a\nclosure literal in `main`, so its driven twin's `yield_now()` sat in a closure\nbody with no frame anywhere — the very cell SL-316 names. Nothing refused it,\nthe yield codegenned to nothing, the twins printed the same thing, and all 320\npairs of that context scored CLEAN on a miscompile. The wrapper now expresses\nwhat its name says (design 77 DF-C1): the closure is created in a body that\nreally is driven, the suspension is in `main`, and the closure is invoked after\nit. `corodiff --all --filter closure_from_driven`: 320 pairs, 0 findings.\nNothing added to `corodiff_known.txt` — the old shape was not a defect to pin,\nit was a wrapper that had stopped expressing a legal program.\n\n--------------------------------------------------------------------\n[P1-b] POST-LOWERING TOTALITY CHECK (b) SEES GENUINE METHOD CALLS\n\nMECHANISM. The `MethodCall` loop asked `ledger.key_of(mc)` — which answers None\nfor a genuine method BY DESIGN, because a method is not a free-function key\nquestion — and then tested only `is_chan_recv`. The check that exists to prove\nno `frame_boundary` call survives as a plain call in a resume body therefore saw\nfree calls, module-qualified free calls wearing a `MethodCall`'s shape, and\nchannel receives, and nothing else.\n\nFIX. Route genuine methods through `ledger.method_target(mc)`, the ledger's one\nmethod decision, and raise on BOTH non-`none` answers: an EMBED that was never\nembedded and an UNSUPPORTED that was never refused reach the executor the same\nway. No allowlist is owed for what a resume body may legitimately call — the\nsub-frame `resume`/`__release` and `Slot`/runtime helpers are SYNTHESIZED after\ndiscovery, so `method_target` answers `none` for them twice over (their\nreceivers carry no resolved type, and a frame struct is in neither method\ncensus). `ledger.method_target` joins the shape table's CONSULTATIONS list so\nthe method arm cannot quietly go away again, and the ledger's consumer census\ncarries the row.\n\nNEGATIVE CONTROLS + PROOF. Three method families, each beside an uninjected\ncontrol (the `icebreadcrumb` pattern), in `tools/test_coro_shapes.py` check 7:\n  - INSTANCE `Counter.step` (`examples/coro_nested_suspend_method.saw`) — ICE\n    names `Counter.step`, `Counter_step` and `__Frame_…`;\n  - STATIC `Napper.doubling` (`examples/coro_static_method_suspends.saw`);\n  - GENERIC-struct with the frame key STAMPED by discovery, `Box2.describe`\n    (`examples/conformance/K34_suspending_generic_struct_method_embedded.saw`).\nThe node is CAPTURED from a real compile at the moment the ledger classifies it\n(a second injection, into `method_target`) and grafted back into a generated\nresume body — a hand-built `MethodCall` would test the check against something\nthe compiler never produced; `_injected` now takes SEVERAL patches for that.\nNON-VACUOUS, verified by reverting the transform hunk: all three families then\nslip check (b) and die downstream in codegen with `internal compiler error …\nUndefined variable: c`, which names a source local and no frame at all — exactly\nthe \"it reaches the executor\" outcome the check exists to stop.\n\n--------------------------------------------------------------------\n[P2] THE SL-327 REPAIR VALIDATES IDENTITY, NOT NUMERIC MEMBERSHIP\n\nMECHANISM. `stdcache._pair_is_consistent` accepted a seed key whenever that\nINTEGER existed among the AST's methods, and `_rekeyed_std_seed` preserved a\nsurviving integer unconditionally. Two id generations make an integer name the\nWRONG method as well as no method — codex's probe (`Command.output` at 20,\n`Command.status` at 10, a stale table keyed 10 whose entry reads\n`Command.output`) passed both, leaving `output` unseeded and its causes on\n`status`. A MISDIRECTED seed, worse than a missing one.\n\nFIX. `stdcache` grows the one reading of the question — `seed_identity` (the\nentry's `` `Owner.method` `` short is the stable name behind a generation-bound\nid), `std_method_index`, and `unsound_seed_keys` (key -> why) — and the\npublication gate, the load-time check and the repair all read it, so they cannot\nanswer differently. Both kinds of bad key are gated at PUBLICATION and both\nre-key by identity. An unsound blob ALREADY ON DISK is discarded AND DELETED at\n`load` — the explicit cache fallback: one cold std build, and the `store` that\nfollows writes a sound blob (publication is write-once, so without the delete\nthat key would rebuild cold forever). An UNRESOLVABLE identity is an invariant\nfailure naming the entry, never a silently dropped seed.\n\nAND A WART THAT EXPOSED. `_rekeyed_std_seed` was called AFTER `_filter_std_ast`,\nwhich drops the std files a program does not compile in — so every excluded\nmodule's entry looked like a keying fault. The consequence was live on the\ninherited tip: the repair path was entered on almost every compile and silently\nDROPPED those entries. It now takes the UNFILTERED `reentry_builtin_ast`; both\nASTs are one generation, so an id found there is the id the entry graph's edges\ncarry, and a seeded leaf for a std method that is not compiled in is inert\n(nothing names its id, and it carries no `sync_reason`).\n\nTESTS — A NEW LANE, `stdseed` (`tools/test_std_seed_keying.py`, wired into\n`tools/battery.sh` and CLAUDE.md's stage list; the battery is 31 stages now). A\nwrong seed key costs a DIAGNOSTIC rather than a failure, so no corpus program\ncan see it — the rules are checked directly, on codex's own numbers: a sound\ntable is returned as the very object it was given; a WRONG-METHOD-ID key and a\nMISSING-ID key are each unsound, refused at publication, and re-keyed onto the\nmethod the entry is about; an UNRESOLVABLE identity reports as an internal\ncompiler error naming the entry; and an unsound blob written to a scratch cache\ndir is discarded and deleted by `load` while a sound one loads. NON-VACUOUS:\nreverting the identity comparison to membership fails six of its assertions.\n`examples/errors/thread_body_reports_both_violations.saw` still passes.\n\n--------------------------------------------------------------------\nGATES\n\nPER-COMMIT (each of the three, under the machine-wide suite lock):\n  compiler suite  2577 passed / 10 xfailed / 0 failed\n  freestanding    36 passed across riscv32 + arm64\n\nTERMINAL BATTERY on the final tip `9e9890e5`: `tools/battery.sh` —\n  31 of 31 stages GREEN in 3500s\n  (suite 391s, icebreadcrumb, lexdiff, astdiff, astgraft, transferdecisions,\n   producertaxonomy, corodiscovery, corototality, stdseed, citations,\n   forgetgate, ircontract, preludegate, docverify, moduletag, stdtypes,\n   toolchain, floatvectors, abidoc, bttable, fuzz, corodiff, bench,\n   selfhostlex, minivm, reemit 721s, irdet --all 888s, gmgate, bootstrap 315s,\n   freestanding 74s)\n\n--emit-frame-ledger DUMP DIFF, whole `examples/` corpus as of main (2589\nprograms, each program's dump compared byte for byte, `COMPILE-FLAGS` honoured):\n  vs the INHERITED U2 TIP 9487b190:  0 of 2589 programs differ.\n      So these three commits change no frame decision and no site decision\n      anywhere in the corpus — which is what a refusal/check fix should do.\n  vs MAIN 50a21f4f:  72 of 2589 differ, every one of them U2's own and\n      already listed —\n        59  `decision=declined` -> `decision=template` (U2's listed flip)\n         8  the suspending-recursion refusal's message (§3 ruling 2: names\n            SL-322, anchors at the recursive function)\n         1  coro_existential_only_body_is_not_framed.saw (SL-323 ruling)\n         1  coro_generic_call_in_an_expression_position.saw (SL-326 flip)\n         1  coro_suspending_call_in_inline_catch.saw (shape-table message)\n         1  coro_transform_error_anchors_in_defining_module.saw (message)\n         1  vector_closure_suspending_transform.saw (SL-316; U2 listed it as\n            the corpus cost)\n      Nothing beyond U2's list; no entry is attributable to these commits.\n\nIR BYTE-COMPARE of the design-220 manifests (optimized-IR artifacts, suite run\non each compiler under the lock), tip vs MAIN:\n  1580 shared entries, 0 artifact missing on either side, **0 BYTE MISMATCHES**\n  3 only-on-tip (programs main's compiler refuses — U2's own flips:\n  coro_entry_template_chain_names_a_suspending_callee,\n  coro_existential_only_body_is_not_framed,\n  coro_generic_call_in_an_expression_position)\n\nNo tracker issues filed and nothing closed. Branch parked for the lead.\n","created":"1789964209","id":"c63"}],"created":"1789738775","description":"USER RULING (Sep 18): revisit the coroutine transform's discovery seams as an EPIC rather than continue per-site funnel fixes. Four units, all ruled as the lead recommended: (1) ONE DISCOVERY LEDGER — a single pass computes the closed frame table (key, declaration, really-suspends, buildable, reason) before any body is lowered; classifier, closure walk, generic promotion, rejectors and drive-site rewriting READ it and derive nothing; a battery lane fails on any transform site that reads the raw suspension bit or a symbol name directly (the producer-taxonomy / astgraft precedent). (2) TOTALITY — every statement and expression shape is either splittable or refused by ONE rejector reading ONE shape table; a callee the walk cannot frame is a compile error, never a plain call whose park blocks the executor (closes SL-287, SL-316; the K33-K37 XFAIL family are rows). (3) COVERAGE UNITS INSIDE THE EPIC — SL-215 (inline try/catch split) and SL-317 (iterator state in the frame for a collection for) are epic units, NOT standalone point fixes; sawtracker's build (currently stopped at those two) is the epic's acceptance test and WAITS for it — the user ruled a point fix is a waste of time. (4) A THREE-VALUED ANSWER AT THE EFFECT SOURCE — the graph answers once (really suspends / only through a closure call / does not) instead of one bit meaning might plus design 206's second question bolted beside it. Census (Sep 18): coro_transform.py 12,224 lines, a 212-method _FrameBuilder, a 1,032-line transform_program with nine nested closures, ~20 distinct suspension/frame-key predicates, 7 split shapes (if, if-let, guard-let, while, RANGE for, match, BLOCK try-catch), 21 scattered refusals, 17 governing designs (44..224). The lowering is stable; every finding this week (SL-280, SL-274 keying sites, SL-306 + its review, SL-215, SL-317, SL-287, SL-316) was a discovery seam. Brief: designs/275-coro-discovery-uniformity.md (lead, next). Sequenced after SL-306.p1 r2 + SL-274.p1 r2 land and the small-fixes batch; ledger unit first.","id":"SL-318","labels":["epic","coro","design-275"],"priority":"high","project":"SL","revision":67,"sequence":1094,"stage":"queued","status":"open","title":"EPIC: coroutine transform discovery uniformity — one frame ledger, total shape coverage, no silent decline (design 275)","updated":"1789964209"},"id":"SL-318","project":"SL","request":"{\"action\":\"patch_revise\",\"actor\":\"agent:claude-sawlang\",\"diff\":\"diff --git a/.claude/skills/saw-lang/SKILL.md b/.claude/skills/saw-lang/SKILL.md\\nindex 0f84254a..54dad33a 100644\\n--- a/.claude/skills/saw-lang/SKILL.md\\n+++ b/.claude/skills/saw-lang/SKILL.md\\n@@ -1046,9 +1046,18 @@ var scratch: [Int8; 256] = [0; 256] // REPEAT literal: N copies of one value\\n   any other. Reading the binding yields the value, so `{ $0 * 2 }` and\\n   `{ $0.to_string() }` are unchanged; what needs the sigil is FORWARDING —\\n   a closure passing its element to another `&T` parameter writes `&n`.\\n-  The closure is not `sync`, so a suspending transform works (the borrow\\n-  spans the suspend; `&self` is held for the whole call, so exclusivity\\n-  forbids the `push` that would reallocate under it). `iter`/`enumerated`\\n+  The closure is not `sync`, but that does NOT make its body drivable: a\\n+  closure is reached through a function value, so its body is not a frame\\n+  and a suspension there has nowhere to park. `v.map({ n in slow(n) })`\\n+  with a suspending `slow` is a clean error at the closure (``appears\\n+  inside a CLOSURE BODY in driven `traverse` ``), wherever the closure is\\n+  written — entry module or a dependency's helper. Four units in order U4 → U1 → U2 → U3: the effect answer at the source, the frame LEDGER + `corodiscovery` gate lane, TOTALITY (no silent decline; SL-287, SL-316, K33–K37), then coverage SL-215 + SL-317 as epic units — NOT point fixes; sawtracker's build waits for the finished foundation (user ruling). Dispatches right after SL-274.p1 lands, AHEAD of the small-fixes batch (user re-sequenced Sep 18); one Opus agent per unit, serially\\n-  - **U1 BUILT (branch `sl318u1`, awaiting the lead's validation + patch).** `sawc/coro_ledger.py::FrameLedger` is the ONE authority for every frame decision — key, kind, cause set, buildable-with-reason, home module, splice origin — built by `_build_frame_ledger` (a worklist to stabilization, extracted out of `transform_program`) and FROZEN before any body is lowered; a write after the freeze and a MISS at a consumer both raise `LedgerMiss`, which `sawc.py` reports as an internal compiler error naming the key. Nineteen consumers routed (the census table is the ledger's docstring); `self._suspends` and the two `typechecker._*_suspending_methods_set` censuses are GONE, and `frame_keys.callee_frame_key` + `coro_ledger.method_frame_key` survive as the ledger's private composers — the transform no longer imports either. `--emit-frame-ledger` dumps both tables deterministically, and the `corodiscovery` battery lane (`tools/test_coro_discovery.py`, 9 checks — seven parse the module, two RUN the compiler) fails on any raw discovery read outside the builder. BEHAVIOUR-PRESERVING, measured: the dump is byte-identical across 2597 programs (examples + blade + libs + selfhost + devtools + the read-only sawtracker sources) before and after, and the design-220 optimized-IR artifacts match the pinned base (8d752a78) on all 1579 rows. CODEX r1 (SL-318.p4) requested changes on two gaps, both fixed in the branch's fifth commit: [P1] the miss invariant was not on the consumer path — the decision tables were filled only under `--emit-frame-ledger` and `free_call_frame` still turned a table miss into `None`, so a gap and a decided negative were one fact; the decision is THREE-VALUED now (`framed` / `no-frame-owed` / `declined(reason)`), recorded on every compile, and `free_call_frame` + `method_target`'s EMBED answer raise `LedgerMiss` naming the key, with the per-read absence-or-miss verdict written into every docstring and a lane check that DROPS one decision and requires the named-key ICE; [P2] driven METHOD ROOTS were missing from the SITE census (their FRAME row appeared beside `# sites: 0`), so discovery now resolves each root's declaration after the walk into a table of its own and the census walks it — nine corpus files gained `context=driven root` rows, six of them from zero, and the eleven bogus \\\"root whose AST no method table holds\\\" declines became real framed rows. U2's worklist, read off the dumps: 67 declined FRAME rows (49 generic METHOD templates, 15 generic FREE templates — both normal — and THREE the census found the walk never reached) and FOUR declined SITE rows, all in `examples/vector_closure_suspending_transform.saw` and all `context=closure body`: the effect graph gives `traverse` NO cause at all (its suspending calls sit inside `Vector.map`/`each`/`fold` closure literals, which raise the conservative cause on the METHOD and no direct edge on the caller), so the walk never reaches `slow_double`/`slow_show`/`slow_add`, no frame is built, and their `yield_now()` runs outside one — a PASSING corpus test whose own comment names the limitation. SL-316's shape, measured rather than inferred. NOT flipped, per the unit's contract: SL-323/K115's `existential_dispatch` membership, SL-324's `might`-where-a-frame-is-meant read, SL-287/SL-316's declines\\n+  - **U2 BUILT (branch `sl318u2`, awaiting the lead's validation + patch).** TOTALITY: the third outcome is DELETED. `sawc/coro_shapes.py` is the SHAPE TABLE — for every shape a suspension can sit in (eight containers, six heads, the statement forms, design 224's expression matrix) it says SPLIT (which `_split_*`, under which stamp), HOIST, INLINE, EMBED or REFUSE (one rejector, one message per row, each naming the issue that owns the missing split) — and `_collect_calls` consults it instead of the hand-written `isinstance` chain that was one of three independent enumerations of the same fact. A container that owns a block and has no row is an INVARIANT failure naming the AST class. TWO TOTALITY CHECKS beside it: (a) after the site census, a re-walk with an INDEPENDENT suspension predicate requires a recorded decision for every suspension position, and asks the table about every container it passes — so a NEW AST shape fails coverage until the table classifies it; (b) after every resume body is generated, a walk asserts that no `frame_boundary` callee survives as a plain call, naming the frame and the callee — `closure_calls_permitted`'s question made total and run for every frame, which is the runtime-facing half. `declined` is GONE from both ledger tables: a generic template is `template` (64 of U1's 67 rows — it owed no frame in the first place), a callee that owes one and has none is `refused`, and a site the transform will not lower is `refuse(<why>)`. THE FLIPS, each with its row: SL-287 (`FrameLedger.unbuildable_callee` is read by the ONE rejector, so a call to a frameless suspending callee is a compile error carrying the ledger's recorded reason); SL-316 (a closure body that really suspends is refused AT THE CLOSURE wherever written — the question is asked of the closure's own effect node, not of its caller, so the entry/imported divergence is gone); SL-326 + SL-330 (the generic promotion walked a positional guess and a `FunctionCall` shape; it walks every position and both call shapes now, so an ANF-hoisted generic call and a module-qualified one are promoted and embedded); SL-331 (consumption symmetry's two questions deadlocked across a TEMPLATE CHAIN — the candidate closure runs to a fixpoint before the `live` test now, which is the mechanism rather than the found symptom, probed: the issue's namespace hypothesis was wrong); SL-324 + SL-325 (three `might`-where-a-frame-is-meant reads flipped to `frame_boundary`, all three strictly widening). Conformance rows K118-K121; the `corototality` battery lane (`tools/test_coro_shapes.py`, six checks — three compare the table against `ast_walk`'s two enumerations and the transform's consumers, three INJECT the failure and require the named ICE, each beside an uninjected control). CORPUS COST, listed not absorbed: `examples/vector_closure_suspending_transform.saw` CHANGED OUTCOME to a refusal (design 216's real claim is covered by `vector_closures_over_nocopy_elements.saw`) and `examples/modules/sl306mod`'s three unused `each_yield_*` helpers moved to `sl316closure`, a fixture that exists to be refused. TWO USER RULINGS folded in Sep 20 before submission: **SL-323** — `existential_dispatch` MOVES OUT of `frame_boundary` into the conservative half beside `closure_call`, so the boundary is `cooperative | blocking | test_suspend` and a body whose only cause is a dispatch is not framed (design 223's refusal AT the dispatch is what keeps it sound — it fires before any framing question, so every dispatch that reaches one has all-sync implementations); `CONSERVATIVE_CAUSES` states the membership once, `conservative_only` joins the derivations beside `closure_only`, the K115 pin flips XPASS and its INDEX row is rewritten to the ruled answer, spec + skill updated. **SL-327** — REPRODUCED and fixed at the KEYING, no change to settling. The symptom is cache-state dependent, which is why it looked environmental: a FRESH std build agrees with itself, and five of eight cached blobs on this machine held a seed table keyed to an AST that was not the one stored beside them (`Command.output` keyed 20761 in the table and 62190 in both the AST and the namespace symbol), every one written by a process whose id bound was an order of magnitude past a fresh std build's — the state `_prepared_builtins`' own docstring says the pair must not be stored from. TWO keying fixes: `stdcache.store` refuses to publish an inconsistent pair, and `sawc._rekeyed_std_seed` re-keys a restored table onto the AST the compile actually holds (by `(owner, method)`, the granularity every other std census already uses), so blobs already on disk are harmless. Proven against the real bad blob: 18 dangling keys -> 0, and the namespace symbol's id seeded after and not before. `examples/errors/thread_body_reports_both_violations.saw` is the standing test, and it FLAPPED before the fix — passing on a freshly built cache and failing on a restored one, minutes apart in one tree. **Design 275 §3 RULING 2 built at the lead's validation:** the two call shapes design 44's by-value embedding cannot serve are rows in the shape table (`coro_shapes.PENDING_SL322`) and each refusal NAMES SL-322 — the K37 dispatch hint keeps DF-223b and adds the pending note, and the suspending-recursion message adds it too AND moved its anchor to the RECURSIVE FUNCTION's declaration (the note SL-280's landing asked for; it used to point at whichever root seeded the cycle walk, e.g. `func main`). Both messages render the note from the one row, both pins assert `SL-322`, the recursion pin pins the anchor line, and the `corototality` lane fails if the row, the raiser and the pin stop agreeing. Conformance row K122. NOT touched, per the unit's contract: SL-215's and SL-317's missing splits (U3's), SL-322's heap frames themselves. U2's worklist, read off the dumps: 67 declined FRAME rows (49 generic METHOD templates, 15 generic FREE templates — both normal — and THREE the census found the walk never reached) and FOUR declined SITE rows, all in `examples/vector_closure_suspending_transform.saw` and all `context=closure body`: the effect graph gives `traverse` NO cause at all (its suspending calls sit inside `Vector.map`/`each`/`fold` closure literals, which raise the conservative cause on the METHOD and no direct edge on the caller), so the walk never reaches `slow_double`/`slow_show`/`slow_add`, no frame is built, and their `yield_now()` runs outside one — a PASSING corpus test whose own comment names the limitation. SL-316's shape, measured rather than inferred. NOT flipped, per the unit's contract: SL-323/K115's `existential_dispatch` membership, SL-324's `might`-where-a-frame-is-meant read, SL-287/SL-316's declines\\n   - **U4 LANDED Sep 19 (SL-318.p3, merge 668fb0c7).** The answer is a CAUSE SET per node, not the three values the brief drafted (lead amendment, Sep 18: a three-valued answer would have forced each consumer to re-derive its own subset privately, which is the secondary analysis the unit removes). ONE set-union pass over the effect graph (`effects.classify_suspensions`) computes which causes reach each node — `cooperative`, `blocking`, `test_suspend`, `closure_call`, plus `existential_dispatch`, a FIFTH cause the sweep found nobody had classified — and the four walkers it replaces (`suspends`, design 206's `really_suspending`, design 242's `suspends_ignoring_blocking`, SL-306's `suspends_ignoring_closure_calls`) are DELETED, each becoming one of five named derivations whose docstring lists its consumers: `might_suspend`, `wraps_main`, `refused_in_thread_body`, `frame_boundary`, `closure_only`. The std seed carries the SET — and, since the lead's review, a SOURCE PER CAUSE beside it — which fixed a cell nobody had measured (a `Thread.spawn` body was permitted to call a std method that parks cooperatively, because the seeded leaf answered off one representative label; the refusal then had nothing left to name and printed a `<suspension source>` placeholder). Conformance rows K111-K115 are the cause × context matrix. U4's own probe found the cell this had been getting wrong: a std method reaching BOTH causes (`Command.output` — it parks on the child's exit and offloads the pipe drain) was seeded into the entry graph under ONE representative source label, so the thread-body question was answered by whichever label the diagnostic path happened to pick, and a `Thread.spawn { c.output() }` was permitted — it compiled AND RAN, parking cooperatively on a thread with no executor to resume it. Design 206's executor question strikes the test intrinsic (it codegens to nothing and is reached only through an explicit `__saw_drive`, so wrapping `main` in a scheduler nothing cedes to would be pure ceremony); the framing question must not, because design 44 splits a driven body's state machine at it. SL-306's first fix asked the executor question where the framing one belonged and dropped every design-44 test's suspension on the floor — an internal compiler error where the method body had been stripped, a silent sync call where it had not — which is the trap this row exists to keep set. `__Frame_main`'s ABSENCE is the executor gate's answer read off the IR, the same discriminator every `yield_now`-in-main test asserts the presence of |\\n | K114 | CAUSE `closure_call` — the conservative cause refuses a `sync` body and a `Thread.spawn` body, frames NOTHING, and does not wrap `main` | `K114_thread_body_refuses_every_cause_but_blocking.saw` + `errors/sync_context_calls_a_closure_taking_helper.saw` + `K111_suspension_causes_decide_framing.saw` + K105's eleven files | design 275 U4, the other half of the disagreement K113 records, and the one SL-306 landed: a call through a non-`sync` function value says MIGHT, so it is not provably suspension-free (every `sync` context refuses it) and it owns no park (nothing is framed for it). Both readings are correct and they are different questions — which is the whole argument for a cause SET over a single bit, since a body carrying this cause AND a cooperative one is refused and framed alike. The thread-body cell is new here: design 242 ruling 9 permits `blocking` and nothing else, so a closure-taking helper in a `Thread.spawn` body is refused exactly as in any other `sync` context — one derivation, not two predicates that happened to agree |\\n-| K115 | CAUSE `existential_dispatch` — a call through `any Trait` refuses a `sync` body, and (TODAY) is framed although it owns no park | `errors/any_sync_suspend.saw` + `coro_existential_only_body_is_not_framed.saw` (XFAIL, SL-323) | design 275 U4's sweep found a FIFTH cause the four walkers never reconciled: design 223 unit 3's ``a call through `any Trait` dispatch``. A module-qualified free\\n+        call — `mod.g<Int>(x)` — wears a `MethodCall`'s shape and names its\\n+        callee through `module_free_call`, so the same three writes land on\\n+        different fields; one method rather than two because they are one\\n+        operation and a second would be the drift this epic exists to end.\\n         \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n         self._open(\\\"a call rename\\\")\\n+        if hasattr(fc, 'method_name'):\\n+            # The qualified spelling. `module_free_call` is what `key_of`\\n+            # answers with and `resolved_symbol` is what codegen's\\n+            # `_generate_module_function_call` resolves against, so both name\\n+            # the instance or the two disagree.\\n+            fc.module_free_call = mangled\\n+            fc.resolved_symbol = mangled\\n+            fc.type_args = None\\n+            return\\n         fc.name = mangled\\n         fc.resolved_symbol = None\\n         fc.type_args = None\\n@@ -1101,6 +1245,29 @@ class FrameLedger:\\n         node = self._nodes.get(key)\\n         return () if node is None else tuple(e.target for e in node.edges)\\n \\n+    def cycle_member_decl(self, key):\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"The DECLARATION a suspending-cycle member was written as, or None.\\n+\\n+        `_analyze_nesting`'s anchor (design 275 §3 ruling 2, and the note\\n+        SL-280's landing asked for). The cycle walk starts at a ROOT, so the\\n+        anchor it carries is whichever root seeded it — for a `__saw_drive` in\\n+        `main`, `func main`, a line the author cannot act on about a cycle\\n+        several hops away. The cycle's own first member is the function to\\n+        break, and this is what finds it: the free body table for a\\n+        `(\\\"fn\\\", key)` and the method table for a raw node id, which are the two\\n+        spellings `_find_suspending_cycle` returns.\\n+\\n+        ABSENCE IS THE ANSWER: a member this unit holds no declaration for\\n+        leaves the root anchor in place, which is what the diagnostic had\\n+        before.\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        if isinstance(key, tuple) and len(key) == 2 and key[0] == \\\"fn\\\":\\n+            return self.free_or_imported_body(key[1])\\n+        if isinstance(key, int):\\n+            entry = self.methods_by_id.get(key)\\n+            return entry[1] if entry is not None else None\\n+        return None\\n+\\n     def node_label(self, key):\\n         \\\"\\\"\\\"How a node names itself in a diagnostic (the cycle chain).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n         n = self._nodes.get(key)\\n@@ -1115,6 +1282,9 @@ class FrameLedger:\\n     def dump(self):\\n         return render_dump(self._frames.values(), self._sites)\\n \\n-    def declined_sites(self):\\n-        \\\"\\\"\\\"U2's worklist: every site the transform lowers as a plain call.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n-        return [r for r in self._sites if r.outcome.startswith(\\\"declined\\\")]\\n+    def refusal_sites(self):\\n+        \\\"\\\"\\\"Every site the transform REFUSES. U1's twin of this answered for\\n+        `declined` — the sites it lowered as a plain call — and that list is\\n+        empty by construction now: design 275 U2 deleted the outcome, and what\\n+        used to be on it is here.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+        return [r for r in self._sites if r.outcome.startswith(\\\"refuse\\\")]\\ndiff --git a/sawc/coro_shapes.py b/sawc/coro_shapes.py\\nnew file mode 100644\\nindex 00000000..2f177211\\n--- /dev/null\\n+++ b/sawc/coro_shapes.py\\n@@ -0,0 +1,441 @@\\n+\\\"\\\"\\\"design 275 U2 — THE SHAPE TABLE. No silent decline, ever.\\n+\\n+THE GUARANTEE (designs 96 / 101 / 104): a suspending call EMBEDS at any nesting\\n+depth and control-flow position, or ERRORS cleanly — it never silently blocks.\\n+Until this unit there was a THIRD outcome. A callee or a closure body the\\n+discovery walk declined was lowered as a PLAIN CALL, and what happened next\\n+depended on which face of the suspension it was: an io park reached codegen's\\n+out-of-frame fallback and BLOCKED the cooperative executor's own OS thread\\n+(SL-287, measured — not one timer tick fired for the whole block), and a\\n+`yield_now()` was simply dropped (`__saw_drive_steps` reporting 0 where 1 was\\n+owed, SL-316). Nothing said a word at compile time or at run time.\\n+\\n+U1 made every one of those decisions VISIBLE — `FrameLedger` records a\\n+`FrameRow` per callee and a `SiteRow` per suspension position, and\\n+`--emit-frame-ledger` dumps both. U2 deletes the third outcome: for every SHAPE\\n+a suspension can sit in, this table says SPLIT, HOIST, INLINE, EMBED or REFUSE,\\n+and a shape the table does not classify is an INVARIANT FAILURE naming the AST\\n+class — never a plain call.\\n+\\n+THE FUNNEL (obligation 1). This module is the ONE chokepoint every\\n+split / hoist / refuse decision goes through, and the census below names its\\n+consumers. Before it, the same question was answered by a hand-written\\n+`isinstance` chain in `_collect_calls`, a second one in `_hoist_container_heads`,\\n+a third in `ast_walk.control_blocks`, and twenty-one refusal messages scattered\\n+across `coro_transform.py` — which is how a container (`TryCatchExpr`, DF-193a)\\n+and a container HEAD (design 224's `match` scrutinee, DF-224a) each came to be\\n+skipped in silence by a walk that listed every OTHER one.\\n+\\n+  | consumer | what it asks |\\n+  |---|---|\\n+  | `_FrameBuilder._collect_calls` | `container_of` — which blocks to descend, and the REFUSE row for one it must not |\\n+  | `_FrameBuilder._collect_calls` (the invariant) | `unclassified_container` — a container class with no row, ICE naming it |\\n+  | `_FrameBuilder._reject_buried_suspend_call` | `refusal_of` — THE message for a REFUSE row, anchored at the shape's introducer |\\n+  | `_FrameBuilder._hoist_container_heads` | `HEADS` — every head slot design 224 lifts |\\n+  | `coro_transform._verify_site_coverage` | `container_of` + `STATEMENTS` — totality check (a) |\\n+  | `tools/test_coro_shapes.py` (the `corototality` lane) | the whole table, against `ast_walk.CONTAINER_KINDS` |\\n+\\n+THE THREE DISPOSITIONS, and why five names rather than three. The brief's\\n+vocabulary is SPLIT / HOIST / REFUSE, which is the right split for a shape that\\n+CONTAINS a suspension. A shape that IS one needs two more names, because the\\n+transform does two different things with it and the dump already spells them:\\n+`INLINE` (a suspend primitive, a channel receive, a blocking-extern offload —\\n+lowered into THIS frame) and `EMBED` (a callee frame embedded by value, design\\n+44). Both are \\\"the statement is already a state boundary\\\"; keeping them apart is\\n+what lets a SiteRow's outcome be read straight off the table.\\n+\\n+CONTEXTS. A shape's disposition is not the whole answer: the same shape is\\n+SPLIT in a driven body and REFUSE inside a closure literal. `disposition_in`\\n+takes the six contexts `coro_ledger.CONTEXTS` names — the three a FRAME plays\\n+(driven root, embedded, spawned) share one column, and the other three\\n+(closure body, sync body, Thread body) are uniform refusals whose rejector is\\n+named per row.\\n+\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+\\n+from typing import NamedTuple, Optional\\n+\\n+from ast_nodes import (ClosureExpr, ExpressionStatement, ForLoop,\\n+                       GuardLetStatement, IfExpr, IfLetExpr, LetStatement,\\n+                       MatchExpr, ReturnStatement, TryCatchExpr, TryExpr,\\n+                       WhileExpr)\\n+\\n+\\n+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\\n+# The dispositions\\n+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\\n+\\n+SPLIT = \\\"SPLIT\\\"      # CFG-split into resume states by the named `_split_*`\\n+HOIST = \\\"HOIST\\\"      # lifted to a driven statement by the named hoist (design 120/224)\\n+INLINE = \\\"INLINE\\\"    # the suspension lowers into THIS frame (no callee frame)\\n+EMBED = \\\"EMBED\\\"      # the callee's frame is embedded by value and driven (design 44)\\n+REFUSE = \\\"REFUSE\\\"    # one rejector, one message, anchored at the shape's introducer\\n+\\n+DISPOSITIONS = (SPLIT, HOIST, INLINE, EMBED, REFUSE)\\n+\\n+\\n+class ShapeRow(NamedTuple):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"One SHAPE: a syntactic slot a suspension can sit in, and its answer.\\n+\\n+    `node` is the AST class name and `slot` the field — together they are the\\n+    shape's NAME, which is what a conformance row and a SiteRow's reader can\\n+    both say. `handler` names the routine that implements the disposition, so a\\n+    reader of the table can go straight to it; `guard` names the stamp the\\n+    transform must find before a SPLIT row applies (a `None` guard is\\n+    unconditional). `reason` and `issue` belong to a REFUSE row and are what the\\n+    one rejector renders.\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    node: str\\n+    slot: str\\n+    disposition: str\\n+    handler: str\\n+    guard: Optional[str] = None\\n+    unguarded: Optional[str] = None     # SPLIT rows: why an unstamped one refuses\\n+    reason: Optional[str] = None        # REFUSE rows: the message body\\n+    issue: Optional[str] = None         # REFUSE rows: the tracker id pending it\\n+    template: Optional[str] = None      # REFUSE rows: the exact text, `{name}`/`{what}`\\n+\\n+\\n+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\\n+# CONTAINER BLOCKS — a suspension written inside a construct's own Block\\n+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\\n+#\\n+# Paired one-for-one with `ast_walk.CONTAINER_KINDS`, and `tools/test_coro_shapes.py`\\n+# fails when the two lists diverge: a container nothing classifies is exactly\\n+# the gap DF-193a and DF-224a were, and it must not be possible to add one\\n+# without answering this table.\\n+\\n+_IFLET_UNGUARDED = (\\n+    \\\"an `if let` / `guard let` whose binding the split preparation \\\"\\n+    \\\"(`_mark_optional_binding_splits`) did not mint a frame field for cannot \\\"\\n+    \\\"become a resume target — its binding exists nowhere else\\\")\\n+\\n+CONTAINERS = {\\n+    IfExpr: ShapeRow(\\\"IfExpr\\\", \\\"then_branch/else_branch\\\", SPLIT, \\\"_split_if\\\"),\\n+    IfLetExpr: ShapeRow(\\\"IfLetExpr\\\", \\\"then_branch/else_branch\\\", SPLIT,\\n+                        \\\"_split_if_let\\\", guard=\\\"_coro_split\\\",\\n+                        unguarded=_IFLET_UNGUARDED),\\n+    GuardLetStatement: ShapeRow(\\\"GuardLetStatement\\\", \\\"else_branch\\\", SPLIT,\\n+                                \\\"_split_guard_let\\\", guard=\\\"_coro_split\\\",\\n+                                unguarded=_IFLET_UNGUARDED),\\n+    WhileExpr: ShapeRow(\\\"WhileExpr\\\", \\\"body\\\", SPLIT, \\\"_split_while\\\"),\\n+    ForLoop: ShapeRow(\\\"ForLoop\\\", \\\"body\\\", SPLIT, \\\"_split_for\\\"),\\n+    MatchExpr: ShapeRow(\\\"MatchExpr\\\", \\\"arms[].body\\\", SPLIT, \\\"_split_match\\\"),\\n+    TryCatchExpr: ShapeRow(\\\"TryCatchExpr\\\", \\\"try_block/catch_block\\\", SPLIT,\\n+                           \\\"_split_try_catch\\\", guard=\\\"_is_split\\\",\\n+                           unguarded=\\\"a `try { } catch { }` that neither spans \\\"\\n+                                     \\\"a suspension nor carries a jump out of an \\\"\\n+                                     \\\"enclosing spanning loop lowers in place\\\"),\\n+    # The two REFUSE rows. Each names the issue that OWNS the missing split, so\\n+    # the message an author reads is a pointer rather than a dead end — and so\\n+    # that the row flips to SPLIT in the landing that closes it (design 275 U3\\n+    # owns both; this unit deliberately adds no split).\\n+    TryExpr: ShapeRow(\\n+        \\\"TryExpr\\\", \\\"catch_block\\\", REFUSE, \\\"_reject_buried_suspend_call\\\",\\n+        reason=\\\"the INLINE `try EXPR catch { ... }` form has no CFG split, so \\\"\\n+               \\\"its catch block cannot become a resume target\\\",\\n+        issue=\\\"SL-215\\\",\\n+        template=(\\n+            \\\"coroutine transform: the suspending call {what} appears in the \\\"\\n+            \\\"catch block of an INLINE `try ... catch {{ ... }}` in driven \\\"\\n+            \\\"`{name}`, and that form has no CFG split — its catch block cannot \\\"\\n+            \\\"become a resume target. Use the BLOCK form \\\"\\n+            \\\"`try {{ ... }} catch {{ ... }}`, which does split, or the \\\"\\n+            \\\"`match` form. (Pending SL-215.)\\\")),\\n+    ClosureExpr: ShapeRow(\\n+        \\\"ClosureExpr\\\", \\\"body\\\", REFUSE, \\\"_reject_buried_suspend_call\\\",\\n+        reason=\\\"a closure body is not driven — it is called through a function \\\"\\n+               \\\"value, and a suspension there has no frame to park in\\\",\\n+        issue=\\\"SL-316\\\",\\n+        # THE WORDING IS LOAD-BEARING and predates this table (design 223 unit\\n+        # 3, widened by SL-306): three `examples/errors/` rows assert\\n+        # `appears inside a CLOSURE BODY in driven `X``, and the message says\\n+        # the limit the author can act on rather than naming two constructs\\n+        # their program does not contain. Moved here rather than reworded, so\\n+        # the table owns the text without the flip costing a diagnostic.\\n+        template=(\\n+            \\\"coroutine transform: the suspending call {what} appears inside a \\\"\\n+            \\\"CLOSURE BODY in driven `{name}`, and a closure body is not \\\"\\n+            \\\"driven — its suspension has no frame to park in. Call it outside \\\"\\n+            \\\"the closure and pass the result in, or move the whole closure \\\"\\n+            \\\"body into a named function the driven body calls.\\\")),\\n+}\\n+\\n+\\n+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\\n+# CONTAINER HEADS — the expression a construct evaluates outside all its blocks\\n+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\\n+#\\n+# design 224 (DF-224a): every one of these is HOISTED into a preceding driven\\n+# `let` by `_hoist_container_heads`, and a head that still spans a suspension\\n+# when `_collect_calls` runs is refused by `_reject_container_head` — never\\n+# descended past, which is what left a `Channel.receive()` in a `match`\\n+# scrutinee spinning at 100% CPU. Paired with `ast_walk.CONTAINER_HEADS`.\\n+\\n+HEADS = {\\n+    (IfExpr, \\\"condition\\\"): ShapeRow(\\\"IfExpr\\\", \\\"condition\\\", HOIST,\\n+                                    \\\"_hoist_container_heads\\\"),\\n+    (WhileExpr, \\\"condition\\\"): ShapeRow(\\\"WhileExpr\\\", \\\"condition\\\", HOIST,\\n+                                       \\\"_hoist_suspending_conditions\\\"),\\n+    (IfLetExpr, \\\"optional_expr\\\"): ShapeRow(\\\"IfLetExpr\\\", \\\"optional_expr\\\", HOIST,\\n+                                           \\\"_hoist_container_heads\\\"),\\n+    (GuardLetStatement, \\\"optional_expr\\\"): ShapeRow(\\n+        \\\"GuardLetStatement\\\", \\\"optional_expr\\\", HOIST, \\\"_hoist_container_heads\\\"),\\n+    (ForLoop, \\\"iterable\\\"): ShapeRow(\\\"ForLoop\\\", \\\"iterable\\\", HOIST,\\n+                                    \\\"_hoist_container_heads\\\"),\\n+    (MatchExpr, \\\"matched_expr\\\"): ShapeRow(\\\"MatchExpr\\\", \\\"matched_expr\\\", HOIST,\\n+                                          \\\"_hoist_suspending_match\\\"),\\n+}\\n+\\n+\\n+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\\n+# STATEMENT shapes — the suspension IS the statement\\n+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\\n+#\\n+# The three forms every classifier in `coro_transform.py` matches, spelled once\\n+# here: `let x = <call>`, a bare `<call>` (or `let _ = <call>`), and — after\\n+# design 83's tail normalization — `return <call>`. Which of INLINE and EMBED a\\n+# row gets is decided by the CALLEE, not by the statement, so both live on the\\n+# one row and `site_outcome` picks.\\n+\\n+_BLOCKING_EXTERN_ROW = ShapeRow(\\n+    \\\"LetStatement\\\", \\\"value (blocking extern)\\\", INLINE, \\\"_emit_blk_call\\\")\\n+\\n+# The one cell of the context matrix a `Thread.spawn { }` body PERMITS rather\\n+# than refuses (design 242 ruling 9) — see `disposition_in`.\\n+BLOCKING_IN_THREAD_BODY = _BLOCKING_EXTERN_ROW\\n+\\n+STATEMENTS = (\\n+    ShapeRow(\\\"LetStatement\\\", \\\"value\\\", EMBED, \\\"_classify_call/_classify_method_call\\\"),\\n+    ShapeRow(\\\"ExpressionStatement\\\", \\\"expression\\\", EMBED,\\n+             \\\"_classify_call/_classify_method_call\\\"),\\n+    ShapeRow(\\\"ReturnStatement\\\", \\\"value\\\", EMBED,\\n+             \\\"_classify_call/_classify_method_call\\\"),\\n+    ShapeRow(\\\"ExpressionStatement\\\", \\\"expression (suspend primitive)\\\", INLINE,\\n+             \\\"_suspend_to\\\"),\\n+    ShapeRow(\\\"LetStatement\\\", \\\"value (Channel.receive)\\\", INLINE, \\\"_emit_recv_call\\\"),\\n+    _BLOCKING_EXTERN_ROW,\\n+)\\n+\\n+_STATEMENT_SLOTS = {\\n+    LetStatement: \\\"value\\\",\\n+    ExpressionStatement: \\\"expression\\\",\\n+    ReturnStatement: \\\"value\\\",\\n+}\\n+\\n+\\n+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\\n+# EXPRESSION shapes — design 224's matrix, one row\\n+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\\n+#\\n+# design 120 gave EVERY expression position an ANF hoist and design 224 widened\\n+# it to container heads and container literals. One row rather than thirty,\\n+# because the hoist is ONE pass with one rule (lift the suspending subexpression\\n+# into a preceding driven `let`, preserving evaluation order and short-circuits)\\n+# and the thirty positions are its input, not thirty decisions. A position the\\n+# hoist cannot lift reaches `_reject_buried_suspend_call`, which is the same one\\n+# rejector this table's REFUSE rows use.\\n+\\n+EXPRESSIONS = ShapeRow(\\\"Expression\\\", \\\"any operand / argument / receiver / \\\"\\n+                                     \\\"interpolation / short-circuit RHS\\\",\\n+                       HOIST, \\\"_anf_hoist\\\")\\n+\\n+\\n+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\\n+# The read API\\n+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\\n+\\n+class UnclassifiedShape(Exception):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"A container the shape table has no row for hosts a suspension.\\n+\\n+    An INVARIANT FAILURE, never a soft answer — the whole point of the table is\\n+    that a NEW AST shape fails the coverage check until it is classified, rather\\n+    than joining `_collect_calls`'s `isinstance` chain's silent fall-through.\\n+    `sawc.py` reports it through `_report_ice`, so it reads as design 192 unit\\n+    2's ordinary internal-compiler-error line with the AST class in it.\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+\\n+\\n+def container_of(ctrl):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"The row for the control-flow construct `ctrl`, or None when `ctrl` is not\\n+    a container at all (a leaf statement, an ordinary expression).\\n+\\n+    THE dispatch `_collect_calls` runs on. A `None` answer for a node that owns\\n+    a `Block` is the invariant failure `unclassified_container` raises.\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    return CONTAINERS.get(type(ctrl))\\n+\\n+\\n+def unclassified_container(ctrl, owns_blocks):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"Raise when `ctrl` owns blocks and this table classifies nothing for it.\\n+\\n+    `owns_blocks` is the caller's `ast_walk.control_blocks(...)` answer, passed\\n+    in rather than recomputed so the two enumerations are compared rather than\\n+    trusted: a construct `control_blocks` descends and this table does not name\\n+    is precisely the gap the unit exists to close.\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    if not owns_blocks or type(ctrl) in CONTAINERS:\\n+        return\\n+    raise UnclassifiedShape(\\n+        f\\\"the coroutine shape table classifies no disposition for \\\"\\n+        f\\\"`{type(ctrl).__name__}`, which owns a block a suspension was found \\\"\\n+        f\\\"in. Every shape a suspension can sit in is SPLIT, HOIST, INLINE, \\\"\\n+        f\\\"EMBED or REFUSE — a shape with no row is a coverage gap, never a \\\"\\n+        f\\\"plain call (design 275 U2). Add its row to `sawc/coro_shapes.py`.\\\")\\n+\\n+\\n+def head_of(owner, field):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"The row for one container HEAD slot, or None.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    return HEADS.get((type(owner), field))\\n+\\n+\\n+def statement_slot(stmt):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"The field of `stmt` a top-level suspending call sits in, or None.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    return _STATEMENT_SLOTS.get(type(stmt))\\n+\\n+\\n+def refusal_of(row, name, what):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"THE message a REFUSE row renders, anchored at the shape's introducer.\\n+\\n+    ONE text per row, built here so the twenty-one scattered refusal messages\\n+    the design-275 census counted cannot grow a twenty-second that words the\\n+    same limit differently. `name` is the driven body's, `what` names the\\n+    offending call the way the author wrote it. A row without a `template`\\n+    gets the generic sentence, which names the shape, the reason and — when the\\n+    missing capability has an owner — the issue that is pending it, so a\\n+    refusal is a pointer rather than a dead end.\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    if row.template:\\n+        return row.template.format(name=name, what=what)\\n+    tail = (f\\\" This is pending {row.issue}.\\\" if row.issue else \\\"\\\")\\n+    return (f\\\"coroutine transform: the suspending call {what} appears in a \\\"\\n+            f\\\"`{row.node}` {row.slot} inside driven `{name}`, and {row.reason}.\\\"\\n+            f\\\"{tail}\\\")\\n+\\n+\\n+def unbuildable_message(name, callee, reason, issue=None):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"THE message for a callee the ledger recorded as UNBUILDABLE — SL-287.\\n+\\n+    The third outcome's own row. A callee whose frame discovery could not build\\n+    used to be lowered as a PLAIN CALL from inside a driven body: the park then\\n+    reached codegen's out-of-frame fallback and stopped the cooperative\\n+    executor's own OS thread, or a `yield_now()` was dropped and the task never\\n+    ceded. Neither said anything. The refusal names the callee AND the ledger's\\n+    own recorded reason, because \\\"no frame was built\\\" without the why is the\\n+    diagnostic that sent three previous readers to the wrong end of the\\n+    pipeline.\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    tail = (f\\\" This is pending {issue}.\\\" if issue else \\\"\\\")\\n+    return (f\\\"coroutine transform: the suspending call `{callee}(...)` inside \\\"\\n+            f\\\"driven `{name}` has no coroutine frame to embed — {reason}. A \\\"\\n+            f\\\"suspending call embeds or errors; it never silently blocks \\\"\\n+            f\\\"(designs 96/101/104).{tail}\\\")\\n+\\n+\\n+def disposition_in(row, context):\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"The disposition of `row` in one of `coro_ledger.CONTEXTS`.\\n+\\n+    The three FRAME roles — driven root, embedded, spawned — share the row's own\\n+    answer: a frame is a frame, and design 44 embeds a callee's by value\\n+    whichever role its caller plays. The other three are refusals and each names\\n+    the rule that refuses it, so the matrix has a value in every cell rather\\n+    than a blank the reader has to interpret.\\n+\\n+    ONE CELL IS NOT A REFUSAL, and it is the exception design 242 ruling 9\\n+    wrote: a `Thread.spawn { }` body may call a `blocking` extern, which runs as\\n+    a PLAIN CALL there and blocks that thread on purpose (the reason to spawn\\n+    one). It is not a shape exception — every shape in this table is refused in\\n+    a thread body — it is a CAUSE exception, and `BLOCKING_IN_THREAD_BODY`\\n+    records which row it lands on so the matrix does not quietly overstate the\\n+    rule.\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    if context in (\\\"driven root\\\", \\\"embedded\\\", \\\"spawned\\\"):\\n+        return row.disposition\\n+    if context == \\\"closure body\\\":\\n+        return REFUSE           # this table's ClosureExpr row\\n+    if context == \\\"sync body\\\":\\n+        return REFUSE           # the typechecker's `sync` check\\n+    if context == \\\"Thread body\\\":\\n+        if row is BLOCKING_IN_THREAD_BODY:\\n+            # design 242 ruling 9: a plain call, blocking that thread.\\n+            return INLINE\\n+        return REFUSE\\n+    raise UnclassifiedShape(\\n+        f\\\"the coroutine shape table knows no context `{context}` \\\"\\n+        f\\\"(design 275 U2; the six are `coro_ledger.CONTEXTS`)\\\")\\n+\\n+\\n+def all_rows():\\n+    \\\"\\\"\\\"Every row, for the gate lane and for the dump's header.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n+    return (tuple(CONTAINERS.values()) + tuple(HEADS.values())\\n+            + tuple(STATEMENTS) + (EXPRESSIONS,) + PENDING_SL322)\\n+\\n+\\n+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\\n+# The two CALL SHAPES design 44's by-value frame embedding cannot serve\\n+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------- #\\n+#\\n+# design 275 §3 RULING 2 (user, Sep 18). Neither of these is a position — they\\n+# are shapes of the CALLEE, and both are REFUSE for the same structural reason:\\n+# a frame is a compile-time identity embedded BY VALUE in its caller's, and\\n+# neither of these has one. An `any Trait` dispatch has a vtable word where the\\n+# identity would be; a suspending CYCLE has no finite embedded size at all.\\n+#\\n+# They are rows here because the ruling is that the refusal is PENDING, not\\n+# permanent: both need a heap-allocated frame with an indirect resume, which is\\n+# one design and one carrier — SL-322 — so neither lands alone. The table\\n+# records that, each message names it, and `tools/test_coro_shapes.py` fails if\\n+# the row and the message stop agreeing.\\n+\\n+PENDING_SL322 = (\\n+    ShapeRow(\\n+        \\\"AnyTraitDispatch\\\", \\\"a suspending conformance body\\\", REFUSE,\\n+        \\\"typechecker._report_existential_suspend_dispatch\\\",\\n+        reason=\\\"a frame is a compile-time identity and dynamic dispatch has \\\"\\n+               \\\"only a vtable word, so the callee's frame cannot be embedded \\\"\\n+               \\\"in the caller's\\\",\\n+        issue=\\\"SL-322\\\"),\\n+
